[[Adana]] [[Bolu]] [[Kütahya]] 4) Lütfiye İnsen Ceritoğlu Kurt [[Pow-pow-pow-pow]] [[Sivas]] [[İstanbul]] [[Inconceived]] [[Inconceived]] [[Gaziantep]] [[Çanrı]] 11) Süzeyya Sadi Bilgiç [[Isparta]] [[Inconceived]] [[Inconceived]] [[İstanbul]] [[İstanbul]] [[Army]] [[Malatya]] [[Erzincan]] [[İstanbul]] [[Denizli]] [[Inconceived]] [[Rize]] Cancer is an important public health problem in the world and in our country. Cancer is a separate cause of death in the world and in our country is the second cause of death after cardiovascular disease. About 170,000 people are diagnosed with cancer each year in Turkey. In 2014, 77,587 people died of cancer. It is clear that cancer deaths that affect a significant number of our people should be dealt with carefully for our country, given its family and environment. Despite significant steps have been taken in our country in the fight against cancer, shortages are being observed in the management of cancer patients who are waiting to die. The last days of the individual's life, the last months, are terminal periods. During the terminal period, individuals' psychological, sociological views and expectations of other people and events change, and their approach to society differs. It's not easy to understand a person who's having a very serious spiritual change, to take the right approach to it. Taking unnecessary tests and treatments, needless attempts, dying in intensive care, away from patients and loved ones, are the main problems of terminal-term patients. Death has to be human. A good death for the patient should be without pain, with family and loved ones, with respect and dignity, and where he chooses. Special care for the terminal period allows the person who needs care to be given short - purposed planning and sharing feelings for the remaining life expectancy. This care should be given especially at home, if not at private palliative care centres in our hospitals or at specialized centres. Scientific research shows that the services provided during the terminal period are cost-effective, reducing emergency applications, hospital applications. In developed countries, a significant percentage of cancer patients (80 percent) die in their homes. The issue was raised in the Symposium of Patient Studies held in Ankara on April 1-3, 2015, making it a report of problems delivered by cancer patients and relatives. The report found that problems with palliative care, lack of psychosocial support, broken family relations in the treatment process, poor post-treatment psychological support are often included. For all these reasons, I would like to announce the opening of Parliamental research in accordance with Articles 98 of the Constitution, 104th and 105th of the Civil Charter, in order to determine the necessary measures for the problems of cancer patients who are one of our country's major health problems during the terminal period and the problems of their relatives. [[Bolu]] [[Rize]] [[Kütahya]] [[Çanrı]] [[Sivas]] [[İstanbul]] [[Inconceived]] [[Inconceived]] [[Malatya]] [[Inconceived]] [[Inconceived]] [[Gaziantep]] [[Inconceived]] [[İstanbul]] [[İstanbul]] [[İstanbul]] [[Denizli]] 18) Süzeyya Sadi Bilgiç [[Isparta]] [[Erzincan]] [[Inconceived]] [[Army]] The climate change, which is expected to lead to major changes in the future of the earth, is one of the issues that are often on our country's agenda. In addition to the industrial revolution, human activities such as the excessive use of fossil fuels, land use changes, deforestation and industrialization have led to a new era in which human activities, for the first time since the middle of the 19th century, have also played a role in climate change. Under global climate change, sectors such as health, forestry, biodiversity, tourism, mainly water resources and agricultural production are expected to be affected. Scientific studies in the international arena indicate that in this century, extreme rains can increase the frequency and intensity of floods, while water quality decay, water pollution, water shortages, and loss of life and property may increase. As of 2080, the same reports indicate that 20 per cent of the world's population will live in basins where the floods of climate change have increased, reporting that hundreds of years of frequency floods will be much more frequent. Currently, hydro-meteorological disasters cause the death of more than 300,000 people worldwide, 325 million people, and economic loss of $125 billion each year. Turkey's impact on climate change is consistent with many other regions around the world. More frequent, severe and long-term droughts, heat waves and forest fires are expected in Turkey in the 21st century. In addition, the number of days of short-term but heavy showers is expected to increase, with significant increases in sudden floods. The reasons for our country's presence in the Eastern Mediterranean Basin, the presence of Mediterranean climate properties in its vast areas, the presence of low-reach coastal areas and areas prone to natural disasters, the presence of droughts and desertification areas, the unique variety of wetlands, mountains, forests, and mountain forest ecosystems, are among the countries at high risk from the negative effects of climate change. In addition to intense impacts, Turkey is extremely vulnerable to possible climate change impacts, according to the Intergovernmental Climate Change Panel (IPCC)'s fourth assessment report. According to a report published by the World Bank (2009), Turkey will be the third most exposed to extreme climate events in Europe and the Central Asia region towards the end of the 21st century. A study conducted by the European Environment Agency (ECHA) envisions water stress in Turkey by more than 40 per cent in the interior and western regions, while in Southeast and Eastern regions, it will be 20 to 40 per cent. These results show that when appropriate measures are not taken in the coming years, there will also be increased loss of life and property due to floods and droughts. The impacts of climate change will affect developing countries, especially economies based on agriculture; they will deepen the problems that already exist here. As reported above, climate change will first increase pressure on water resources in our country. Our water sources are already under pressure for industrialization, rapid urbanization, consumption policies. Because climate change is not just an environmental problem, political decisions are decisive in determining the solution or course of the issue, as there are socioeconomic consequences. There is a need for scientific-based and national-sized research on how much we need to do to prevent the deepening of this problem, and for measures to be implemented in its results. After the 2001 crisis, Turkey's economy, which has recovered with the rational and sustainable measures of the EC PARTY and is feeling the effects of the global crisis at a minimum, is growing day by day, and aims to enter among the world's top 10 economies in 2023. In our country, the furniture industry has the appearance of a workshop-type, mostly traditionally employed, that small-scale businesses are heavy. These businesses operate as a family business, and production costs are high because they work inefficiently with low capacity use rates. Industrial production, which began after the 1970s, increased the number of small-scale businesses, as well as medium and large-scale businesses. The sector, which has a size of $6 billion and has greater potential for growth, has cost and quality problems due to the effort to get certain inventory out of the sector, to jump investments, to capacity use, to the lack of knowledge and not majoring in it. Furniture industry has recently been in a rapid transformation and a process of globalization. Facing growing competition in the sector, there are facilities that produce on an economic scale and world standards, and with franchises, the country sells goods to the market and to the world. The export of furniture in Turkey, which has seen rapid growth in recent years, was $1.2 billion in 2009, while in 2012 it was up to $1.899 billion, and by 2013 it was able to increase exports from the sector to 2.2 billion. However, the import of furniture, which was $567.6 million in 2009, was worth $817.3 million in 2012, and $968.6 million in 2013. China received the largest share of furniture exports in 2013. It's followed by Germany, Italy, Poland. Turkey has successfully taken its place as the 15th most furniture exported country in the world from 228 countries. China is leading the world's furniture industry in terms of production, and in the last five years, it has led the industry in various ways. A quarter of the furniture produced worldwide is produced in China alone. China followed by the United States (15 per cent), Italy (8 per cent) and Germany (7 per cent) respectively. In terms of production, Turkey has a 1% rate and is potentially inclined to rise above it. When we look at the percentages of exports, we can say that China is still in first place at 35.3 per cent, respectively, Germany (7.6 per cent), Italy (6.8%), Poland (5.7 per cent), the United States (4.8 per cent) and Mexico (3.9%). As you know, the fact that there is a law authorizing budgets, revenue collections and spending is one of the most important areas in which the legislative body operates the control and control function on the administration. In terms of policy theory, the budget is considered to be the basis of democracy and the separation of forces, both theoretically and in terms of its historical manifestation. In this context, the temporary budget, as a concept, is an exceptional situation in the application, which is a unique property to itself. For, with the temporary budget, the government is on the basis of the latest collection and spending power given by the legislature, and it is almost as if the mandate should be extended under certain conditions. In this regard, I would like to stress once again the importance of public financial management and control law No. 5018, among the first generation of AK PARTI reforms, which has brought temporary budget implementation into our cognitively legal system. The existence of such a mechanism also protects Turkey from negative situations, such as the government's failure to do business and the failure of public services, as seen in some countries. As they know, as seen in the United States of America in October 2013, the closure of the government has occurred.
